LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 3 ("...I saw something red... from under the cherry tree") and Evidence 7 (Bigg Building Pamphlet). The pamphlet clearly states that the cherry tree is on the viewing platform. Testimony 3 implies Kay saw something red *from* under the cherry tree, suggesting she was *under* it. However, the viewing platform is open and exposed; being "under" the cherry tree would require a significant obstruction or a very low-hanging branch, which isn't indicated in the pamphlet or any other evidence. This creates a spatial inconsistency: Kay's recollection of being under the cherry tree doesn't align with the known layout of the viewing platform.

**Step 2: Check if any generated rules apply**

Rule 8 states: "If XXX was near the cherry tree then XXX was on the viewing platform." This rule directly addresses the spatial relationship. Kay's testimony suggests she was *under* the cherry tree, which implies she was *not* on the viewing platform. This contradicts Rule 8.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Rule 8 directly contradicts Kay's testimony. The rule establishes that proximity to the cherry tree necessitates being on the viewing platform. Kay's statement of being *under* the tree implies a location *not* on the platform, thus violating the rule.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the layout of the Bigg Building's viewing platform as described in Evidence 7. The pamphlet depicts an open platform with a cherry tree on one side. Being "under" the tree is spatially incompatible with being *on* the platform, as the rule dictates.

None of the provided rules directly address the contradiction of an object being *moved* from one location to another and the differing implications of that movement.

The contradiction is identified through understanding the implications of "ripped from" versus "found in." "Ripped from" implies an action, a transfer of the button from Corrida's costume to Engarde's possession (and subsequently, his hakama). "Found in" implies a passive presence, suggesting the button was simply there. The spatial element is the *movement* of the button, and the testimony downplays this movement, creating a contradiction with the evidence. LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Simeon's testimony (Testimony 4) and the evidence related to the Bigg Building (Evidence 2). Simeon states that the "double" (presumably a stand-in for President Wang) entered the hatch to the 51st floor *after* firing a shot at him. However, Evidence 2 states that there is an entrance to the hidden 51st floor on the *roof* of the Bigg Building. This implies the hatch Simeon refers to is on the roof, not a separate entrance. Simeon's testimony suggests a separate, lower-level hatch, which contradicts the established spatial relationship of the Bigg Building.

**Step 2: Check if any generated rules apply**

Rule 3: "If HHH entered the hatch after XXX then XXX exited before HHH" - This rule *could* apply, but it doesn't directly reveal the contradiction. It simply states a temporal relationship, not a spatial one. The core issue is the location of the hatch itself.

Rule 9: "If the XXX was found inside YYY and YYY is connected to ZZZ then the XXX is connected to ZZZ" - This rule is not relevant as it deals with items found *inside* a location, not the location of an entrance.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

While Rule 3 is relevant to the sequence of events, it doesn't directly address the spatial contradiction. The contradiction is not about *who* entered the hatch, but *where* the hatch is located.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the described location of the Bigg Building (Evidence 2) and comparing it to Simeon's description of the hatch (Testimony 4). Evidence 2 establishes the 51st-floor entrance as being on the roof. Simeon's testimony implies a separate, lower-level hatch. These two descriptions are spatially incompatible. Simeon's account doesn't align with the known structure of the Bigg Building.

**Step 2:** **Check if any generated rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Crime Photo 2 (Evidence 3) and Phoenix's Testimony (Testimony 3). Crime Photo 2 states the victim's watch stopped at 3:05. Testimony 3 states that Phoenix shoved the victim, and then the victim fell on top of his umbrella. This implies the events occurred shortly before the time of death. However, the Student's Testimony (Evidence 9) states the power cable broke at 2:55 PM. If the cable broke at 2:55 PM, and the time of death is 3:05 PM, there's a significant time gap. The broken cable is the cause of death (Doug's Autopsy Report - Evidence 1), so the time of the cable break should be closer to the time of death. Phoenix's testimony places the shove and umbrella incident close to the time of death, but the cable break occurred much earlier.

**Step 2: Check if any generated rules apply**

Rule 5: "If the student saw the cable break at 2:55 then the cable was intact before 2:55" - This rule supports the timeline presented by the Student's Testimony.

Rule 3: "If the watch stopped at 3:05 then the time of death is approximately 3:05" - This rule supports the time of death given in the Autopsy Report.

**Step 3: If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Rule 5 and Rule 3 highlight the contradiction. Rule 5 establishes that the cable was intact before 2:55 PM. Rule 3 links the watch stopping at 3:05 to the approximate time of death. Phoenix's testimony places the shove and fall on the umbrella close to the time of death (3:05), but the cable break occurred significantly earlier (2:55). This creates a temporal inconsistency – the cable should have been broken *before* the victim died, not an hour and ten minutes prior.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the sequence of events leading to the death. The autopsy report and student testimony establish a timeline. Phoenix's testimony, when considered alongside these facts, creates a conflict. The spatial relationship of the broken cable above the victim (Crime Photo 1) implies a direct causal link to the death. The significant time difference between the cable break and the estimated time of death, as described in the testimonies and evidence, is the contradiction.

{"evidence": 3, "testimony": 3}

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the temporal relationship between events. The weather data provides a timeline of events (lightning strike at 10:45 PM). Butz's testimony places his actions (hearing thunder and going to the bridge) in relation to that timeline. **Step 3: If applicable rules exist, state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction**

Rule 5 directly reveals the contradiction. The rule states that an unlit lantern creates a dark area. Testimony 2 describes a scenario where Elise is positioned *relative to* the lantern, implying a degree of visibility or spatial awareness facilitated by the lantern. The rule's implication of darkness contradicts this spatial relationship described in the testimony.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the relationship between a person and a light source. Being "with one's back to" a light source implies a certain level of illumination and spatial awareness. The evidence stating the lantern was unlit negates this possibility, creating a spatial inconsistency.

**Step 4: If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The contradiction lies between Testimony 1 (Starbuck) and Evidence 2 (Space Center Diagram). Starbuck states, "Like always, I took the elevator down to the middle level and headed for the corridor." However, Evidence 2 shows that the elevator is between Launch Pad 1 and the third-floor lounge, and the passage connects Launch Pad 1 to the third-floor lounge. This implies Starbuck would have had to pass through the third-floor lounge to reach the corridor from the middle level of Launch Pad 1 via the elevator. This is spatially inconsistent.

**Step 2: Check if any generated rules apply**

Rule 1: If the elevator is between XXX and YYY then the ladder is not between XXX and YYY. This rule doesn't apply as it concerns the ladder, not the elevator's relationship to the lounge and corridor.
Rule 2: If XXX connects to YYY via a passage then YYY does not connect to XXX via a different passage. This rule *does* apply. The passage connects Launch Pad 1 to the 3rd-floor lounge. Starbuck's testimony implies he used the elevator to get from the middle level of Launch Pad 1 to the corridor, which would necessitate passing through the lounge. This suggests a connection between the middle level of Launch Pad 1 and the corridor *via* the lounge and elevator, contradicting the idea that the passage is the *only* connection.
Rule 3: If the bombing occurred on floor XXX then the evacuation route did not pass through floor XXX. This rule is irrelevant.
Rule 4: If YYY is on the upper level of Launch Pad 1 then XXX is not on the lower level of Launch Pad 1. This rule is irrelevant.
Rule 5: If the bullet hole is in the boarding lounge then the bullet did not originate from the control room. This rule is irrelevant.
Rule 6: If the door lock operates via fingerprint then the door lock does not operate via key. This rule is irrelevant.
Rule 7: If the oxygen tank is ruptured then the oxygen level is not at 80%. This rule is irrelevant.
Rule 8: If the lighter is found with XXX's fingerprints then the lighter is not found with YYY's fingerprints. This rule is irrelevant.
Rule 9: If the "Hope" capsule was in XXX's possession then it was not in YYY's possession. This rule is irrelevant.
Rule 10: If the security camera shows XXX and YYY escaping then the security camera does not show ZZZ and HHH escaping. This rule is irrelevant.

**Step 3: Apply applicable rules**

Rule 2 directly reveals the contradiction. The passage connects Launch Pad 1 to the 3rd-floor lounge. Starbuck's testimony implies he used the elevator, which would require him to pass through the lounge, creating a second connection between Launch Pad 1 and the corridor. This violates Rule 2, which states that if two locations are connected by one passage, they cannot be connected by a different passage.

**Step 4: Spatial Reasoning**

The spatial reasoning involves understanding the layout of the Space Center as depicted in Evidence 2. The elevator's location and the passage's connection are key to identifying the contradiction in Starbuck's testimony. The elevator route implies a different, and therefore conflicting, path than the established passage.

Rule 5: "If XXX is to the right of YYY and YYY is to the right of ZZZ then XXX is to the right of ZZZ" - This rule deals with a right-left relationship, not the state of a person.
Rule 6: "If XXX is above YYY and YYY is below ZZZ then XXX is above ZZZ" - This rule deals with a vertical positioning relationship, not the state of a person.

**Step 4: Spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction**

The contradiction arises from a difference in spatial state. Testimony 3 describes an active, upright position ("standing"), while Evidence 3 describes a passive, prone position ("lying") and a state of being covered ("covered by books"). These descriptions are spatially incompatible; a person cannot simultaneously be standing and lying, nor can they be both upright and covered in books.
